`process` and `reset` are `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`<br>• `core/include/flub/common/Realtime.h`<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/ModuleSlot.h`, `core/src/engine/ModuleSlot.cpp`: dry path delayed by module latency, crossfaded bypass, pre-roll<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/ProcessingChain.h`, `core/src/engine/ProcessingChain.cpp`: sums slot latencies per latency profile; profile-gated modules (the STFT gate is in the chain only in Quality, the precedent for model eligibility)<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/MixEngine.h`, `core/src/engine/MixEngine.cpp`: pads strips to equal latency<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/MacroMap.h`, `core/src/engine/MacroMap.cpp`: table-driven control contributions a model's control outputs could feed<br>**Not present:** any ONNX Runtime dependency or real `ModelRunner`, model manifests, any model (N1–N7), a host that installs a model (the app, plug-in and CLI never call `setNeuralModel()`), parameters or a UI card for the slot, per-band / mask controls, and the neural status and counters in the UI.<br>Round 8 / hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): `app/Source/engine/AudioEngineHost.*` `setNeuralModel` / `clearNeuralModel` (a factory makes a runner for every engine the host builds, installed through `MixEngine`'s `ChainSetup` before `prepare()`, so a model install or removal goes through the crossfaded swap, R1.5); `core/include/flub/neural/AsyncModelProcessor.h` and `ProcessingChain`: offline (waiting) mode and the `BlockTooLarge` status | `tests/test_neural.cpp`, all 14 cases (the real worker thread with scripted stand-in models):<br>• `Neural: latencySamples is frameSize * (1 + safetyFrames) and the identity model delays the input by exactly that`<br>• `Neural: the model receives consecutive input frames as a mono downmix or as planar channels`<br>• `Neural: the control computed from a frame is applied to exactly that frame's samples`<br>• `Neural: a constant -6 dB model scales the delayed input by -6 dB once the control ramp has settled`<br>• `Neural: channel-gain controls scale each output channel separately`<br>• `Neural: a blocked model counts each missed frame, holds the last good control, then crossfades to neutral after K misses`<br>• `Neural: a failing model counts failures, holds the last good control and falls back to neutral`<br>• `Neural: non-finite controls count as failures and controls are clamped to [0, maxGain]`<br>• `Neural: reset() clears the delay and the controls without stopping the worker`<br>• `Neural: process() and reset() are allocation-free`<br>• `Neural: an invalid runner is inert and a sample-rate mismatch keeps the latency without running the model`<br>• `Neural: eligibility follows the latency-profile rule`<br>• `Neural: repeated create, prepare, process and destroy joins the worker cleanly`<br>`tests/test_neural_slot.cpp`, all 10 cases (the chain's neural slot with stand-in models on the real worker thread; green on CI in run 14):<br>• `NeuralSlot: with no model the latency is the reference value per profile and the output bit-identical to an emptied slot's`<br>• `NeuralSlot: an identity model in Quality adds exactly its latency L and delays the chain's output by L`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a 2-frame model is ineligible in Low Latency: slot bypassed, latency unchanged, status says why`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a sample-rate mismatch, an invalid description or a failing prepare keep the slot out with the reason`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a constant -6 dB (or +12 dB) model before the limiter still holds the true-peak ceiling on hot material`<br>• `NeuralSlot: bypassing an active model fades to the latency-compensated dry path without changing the latency`<br>• `NeuralSlot: process() with an active model is allocation-free (bypass toggles, NaN reset and mode switch included)`<br>• `NeuralSlot: clearNeuralModel restores the original latency and output after the next prepare()`<br>`tests/test_rtsan.cpp` (RTSan build only): `hasNonblockingProcess<AsyncModelProcessor>` and `hasNonblockingReset<AsyncModelProcessor>` static_asserts, and `ProcessingChain::setNeuralBypass` / `getNeuralStatus` / `getNeuralCounters` as `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`; the Neural and NeuralSlot cases run under RealtimeSanitizer there.<br>Contracts a neural module relies on:<br>• `tests/test_engine.cpp`: `ModuleSlot: bypassed slot is a pure latency-compensated delay and toggling is click-free`; `Chain: latency per profile and constant under module bypass` (despite its name, it checks only per-profile ordering, the < 5 ms / < 2.5 ms bounds and the re-prepare flag; it does not toggle modules); `Chain: everything bypassed = input delayed by the chain latency (bit-transparent path)`; `MixEngine: strips are summed, padded to equal latency and master-limited`; `Chain: process() is allocation-free in every mode and profile`; `MacroMap: every mode has five named macros that engage modules`<br>• `tests/test_primitives.cpp`: `SpscRing: FIFO order, capacity and overflow drop`<br>• `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`: `RTSan: an allocation inside a nonblocking function stops the process (sanitizer self-test)`<br>The Neural cases are green on CI in run 12 (all four compilers, ASan + UBSan, RTSan).<br>Round 8 / hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16):<br>• `Neural: offline mode runs the model inside process(), so a render faster than real time gets every frame's result`<br>• `NeuralSlot: an Offline render faster than real time applies every frame's model result, reproducibly`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a host buffer longer than the model's safety frames keeps it out in real time (BlockTooLarge)`<br>• `App: neural model install and removal mid-stream go through the swap (model in the new engine, no click, no gap)` | Partially implemented | The neural framework of 09 §1.1 exists in the core and is tested on its real worker thread. Asserted:<br>• `latencySamples()` = `frameSize × (1 + safetyFrames)` for 0, 1, 2 and 5 safety frames; with one safety frame the identity model's output is the input delayed by exactly L, bit for bit, with odd host blocks (1 to 64 samples);<br>• the model sees consecutive frames as the mean of the channels (mono) or per channel (planar);<br>• a model returning 1 / level of its frame leaves every frame at unity within 1e-5 after the ramp, so each control lands on its own frame, not one early or late;<br>• a constant −6 dB model gives −6 dB within 0.01 dB (every sample within 1e-6 of the delayed input × gain); per-channel controls scale each channel;<br>• a model blocked on a gate: each missed frame is counted exactly (8 of 8), the last good control holds for K − 1 misses, then a 48-sample ramp to unity with no step larger than the ramp slope; after release the stale frames are skipped and the model's control returns;<br>• `run()` returning `false` and NaN controls count as failures (exactly 6, 7, 19 and 14 in the scenarios), with the same hold-then-neutral fallback; controls clamp to `[0, maxGain]`; the output stays finite;<br>• `reset()` with a frame in flight clears the delay line, keeps the worker running and calls the runner's `reset()` before the next frame;<br>• `process()` and `reset()` make 0 allocations, including while the model is blocked and the input queue overflows;<br>• a null runner or an invalid description is inert (latency 0, audio untouched); a sample-rate mismatch keeps the latency at unity gain without running the model;<br>• eligibility: Low Latency ≤ 1 reference frame (480 / 481 samples at 48 kHz, 441 / 442 at 44.1 kHz), Balanced ≤ 2, Quality and offline any; invalid latency or rate never;<br>• 50 create / prepare / re-prepare / process / destroy cycles join the worker cleanly; an explicit poll interval is capped at 100 ms.<br>Run 100 times in a row locally without a failure (the waits are on explicit conditions, bounded only as hang guards). The existing extension points still hold: any Processor declares a fixed `latencySamples()`, ModuleSlot keeps bypass latency-constant, `ProcessingChain::getLatencySamples()` sums every slot's latency (1352 / 192 / 100 smp, 28.2 / 4.0 / 2.1 ms at 48 kHz for Quality / Balanced / Low Latency, re-measured with the CLI; the test asserts Balanced < 5 ms and Low Latency < 2.5 ms).<br><br>The chain's neural slot (`tests/test_neural_slot.cpp`, 8 cases, green on CI in run 14; each passed 100 consecutive local runs):<br>• with no model, or a model installed and cleared, the latency is exactly 1352 / 192 / 100 samples (Quality / Balanced / Low Latency at 48 kHz) and the output bit-identical to a chain that never had one;<br>• an identity model with L = 512 in Quality adds exactly 512 samples (latency and the `latencyMs` meter) and the output is the model-free output delayed by L (within 1e-6 with static settings, 1e-2 with the Boost macro);<br>• a 960-sample model is ineligible in Low Latency (slot out, latency and output unchanged, the reason names the latency profile), active in Balanced after a re-prepare, out again in Low Latency, and active in any profile in the Offline context;<br>• a sample-rate mismatch, an invalid description and a runner whose `prepare()` throws each keep the slot out with their reason and a bit-identical output;<br>• a constant −6 dB or +12 dB model ahead of the maximizer keeps the 4× true peak within the ceiling + 0.15 dB and the sample peak at the ceiling, with no safety clip, with the full Music boost and with the maximizer alone (where its effect on the loudness is measurable);<br>• the slot's bypass fades to the latency-compensated dry path (within 1e-6 after the fade) without changing the latency;<br>• `process()` with an active model makes 0 allocations in all three profiles, including bypass toggles, a NaN reset and a mode switch;<br>• `clearNeuralModel()` restores the original latency and a bit-identical output after the next `prepare()`, and the counters restart.<br><br>**Why Partially implemented:** there is no inference runtime and no model. No ONNX Runtime `ModelRunner`, no model manifest and none of N1–N7 exist; the tests use stand-in runners. The chain now has a neural slot that applies `isEligible()` at `prepare()`, but no shipped code installs a model (the app host can now install one through the engine swap, `AudioEngineHost::setNeuralModel`, tested with a reference runner, but nothing in the app, plug-in or CLI loads a model), and the slot has no parameters or UI card. Controls are linear gains only (per-band gains and masks need a band-split renderer), and the slot's status and miss / failure counters (`getNeuralStatus()`, `getNeuralCounters()`) are not shown in the UI.<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): an offline render (batch / export) runs the model inside `process()` so every frame gets its result however fast the render is, and a host buffer longer than the model's safety frames is reported as `BlockTooLarge` instead of counting structural deadline misses. |

Latency is under 6 ms at 22.05 kHz and above and under 12 ms at 8 / 16 kHz (Balanced only; Quality is 30.2 ms at 44.1 kHz and 144 ms at 8 kHz).<br><br>Measured with the CLI and re-measured (Balanced / Low Latency): 8 kHz 92 / 60 smp = 11.5 / 7.5 ms (only 0.5 ms under the 12 ms bound), 16 kHz 7.0 / 4.25 ms, 44.1 kHz 4.13 / 2.18 ms, 48 kHz 4.00 / 2.08 ms, 96 kHz 3.25 / 1.54 ms. 03 §1.4: the Bluetooth preset (−2 dBTP ceiling) reads −2.05 dBTP at worst.<br><br>**Why Partial:**<br>1. Every profile is `labVerified:false`. Neither the device-lab plan in 10 §5 (2 h soak, loopback true peak ≤ cap, hot-plug and sleep ≤ 2 s, A2DP ↔ HFP switch, added latency ≤ 10 / 12 ms) nor roadmap item 1.10 has been run on a real headset, so compatibility rests on standard device classes, not measurement.<br>2. Linux `queryOutputTransport` returns Unknown, so Linux relies on name and format heuristics. The Windows and macOS transport queries are build-only and have never run against a device.<br>3. Applying the cap on a device change is now tested headlessly (`flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11): `EngineController::simulateOutputDevice`, the path a real device change takes (`updateDeviceProfile` → `applyDeviceProfile` → `AudioEngineHost::setMasterCeilingDb`), for a Bluetooth Stealth, a hands-free Stealth (16 kHz mono), a wired Recon, the hands-free one again and a Realtek output sets the connection, the advice and the master ceiling to −2 / −3 / −1 / −3 / −1 dBTP; two full-scale strips pushed about 11 dB over full scale then leave the output sample peak between cap − 0.6 dB and cap + 0.05 dB, with at least 6 dB of master gain reduction. The advice banner, switching back to the preferred output with a 5 s rescan and per-device dismissal have no automated test beyond the screenshot. The master limiter at the −2 / −3 dBTP caps is now tested directly, with the cap taken from `adviceFor()` for a Bluetooth Stealth endpoint (44.1 / 48 kHz) and a hands-free one (8 / 16 kHz) and set with `MixEngine::setMasterCeilingDb` as the app does: on material whose true peak is 9–10 dB over the cap, true peak ≤ cap + 0.1 dB on the 4× meter and an independent 4× interpolator, sample peak ≤ cap, no safety clamp (measured 0.045–0.050 dB below the cap). The air-exciter cut-off is tested too: at 8, 16 and 32 kHz, air at 100 % (also via the Clarity macro) leaves the output equal to the delayed input within 1e−6, while at 44.1 and 48 kHz it adds its harmonics and shelf. What remains untested on the app side is the banner, the preferred-output rescan and per-device dismissal.<br>4. The Game / Chat virtual-endpoint routing in 10 §4 needs the Windows driver / macOS HAL plug-in, which are designed only.<br>5. The headset microphone is not processed, and console-only use is out of scope by design.<br><br>docs/11 E17 (green on CI in run 16): hands-free (HFP) detection now covers rates up to 32 kHz (super-wideband), a name with "wireless" no longer implies USB, and Bluetooth evidence beats a profile's typical connection.<br><br>Wave B2 (local runs only, green on CI in run 17): the user's `device-profiles.json` override is read through `juce::File`, so a user-data folder with a non-ASCII name works on Windows whatever the ANSI code page, and a damaged override falls back to the shipped database. |

## Known gaps

Every row that is not **Implemented and tested**, with what is missing. Details and figures are in each row's Notes.

- **R1.1 Latency (Partially implemented).** The 10–12 ms end-to-end target is not measured. The ≈9.5 ms and ≈12–13 ms figures are estimates for the Windows virtual-driver path, which is design only; there is no loopback measurement (07 item 1.2, 10 §5 test 8), and the UI readout is reported, not measured. Today's Windows process-loopback path alone adds a 612-frame (12.75 ms) FIFO target, and the default Linux PipeWire quantum is ≈21 ms unless forced to 256 (01 §5.3). The docs now share one budget with explicit scopes (01 §5). Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the readout is now per strip, labelled an estimate and "--" without an audible path, and a strip is padded only within its sync group (docs/11 E42a / E40 part 3); the PipeWire quantum is added only when set (`setGraphQuantumMs`), nothing detects it yet.
- **R1.2 Audio backends (Partially implemented).** No test opens a device. CI run 36247109446 built the app and plug-in on Windows (WASAPI), macOS (CoreAudio) and Linux (ALSA / JACK); ASIO has no recorded build (it needs the Steinberg SDK). The native PipeWire `pw_filter` node, per-stream `node.latency`, the exclusive-mode policy (08 D2) and the ASIO single-client note (08 D10) are roadmap, and no device-lab results exist.
- **R1.5 Buffer management (Partially implemented).** Glitch-free operation on the real audio thread is not exercised: the app's callback code is not RTSan-annotated (it is allocation-, free- and, on Linux, lock-tested with a fake device in `flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11), and `DriftCompensatedFifo` is proven in simulation only. The CPU-overload watchdog and the FIFO `Stats` in the UI exist and are tested with chosen samples and fake devices (green on CI in run 12); the opt-in automatic response to an overload (one latency-profile step down, rate-limited, never back up; `tests/app/test_app_overload_response.cpp`, green on CI in run 14) is tested the same way. Neither has seen a real overload. The crossfaded engine swap on structural changes now exists (round 8, green on CI in run 16) and is tested with a fake device, including allocation / free / lock probes over 100 swaps, but not on a real device. Missing: a real-device soak (07 items 1.1, 1.10; the glitch detector and an offline soak with parameter automation exist since docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2, local runs only), RealtimeKit and driver clock slaving (design only). Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the docs/11 E51 loopback guard, the capture restart at a new device rate and the stalled-callback quarantine are tested with fake devices only; the device banner with Retry / Choose output / Sound settings is built since docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (local runs, fake devices), and the device callback's duration / interval histograms (E45) are recorded but not shown.
- **R3.5 Anti-clipping + THD protection (Partially implemented).** The governor now acts on measured THD+N of the saturator and the soft clipper over 25 ms windows (the clipper's share floored at the former clip-energy proxy over the same window, so it does not act later than before), tested as a unit and end to end (`tests/test_distortion.cpp`, green on CI in run 14). What remains is coverage: at the default protection strength (Off) it takes back only governed macro contributions, so distortion from base `max.drive` / `sat.drive` is measured but not limited (strength Normal / Strict also scales the base drives since docs/11 E06's slice, Phase 2 batch 1, local runs only, and since Phase 2 batch 2 (local runs) it is chosen in the app, Settings › Processing or the Boost panel's governor chip; all Music macros 100 on a 50 Hz sine still measure 6.0 % THD+N at Strict against the ≤ 3 % target); the limiter's own gain-modulation distortion is a governor input only at Normal / Strict (docs/11 E06 step 1, Phase 2 batch 2, local runs); the remaining THD+N of the ≤ 3 % scene is the bass harmonics generator's intended harmonics; the bass harmonics generator and the air exciter are measured but deliberately not a governor input (their reading is now published in the meter bus, not shown); and the measured distortion is shown only as the governor chip's ~3 s average (tooltip) and the LoudnessPanel's Distortion / Harmonics bars.
- **R4.1 Dark GUI (verified by build / manual run only).** No tests of the look or layout (`flub_app_tests` checks only the meter views' values, R4.2, and the header / Settings CPU and capture text, R1.5); screenshots are uploaded, not diffed; Windows and macOS rendering are unverified (CI builds the app there but renders nothing). The UI scale setting and the high-contrast theme (roadmap 3.6) now exist, with their contrast ratios, theme switch, settings and minimum window sizes tested in `flub_app_tests` (green on CI in run 16), but not their rendering. Screen-reader live values, onboarding (1.6 / 3.6), localisation (3.7) and the dark plug-in editor (2.9) are missing.
- **R4.4 Tray + global hotkeys (Partially implemented).** Linux global hotkeys are implemented for X11 (`XGrabKey`) and tested under Xvfb (locally, and by the CI `sanitizers` job's Xvfb step, first green in run [36269443100](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/36269443100) on commit `df28c48`, where the X11 case ran rather than skipped). Wayland sessions now use the xdg-desktop-portal GlobalShortcuts interface, tested only against a mock portal on a private `dbus-daemon` (green on CI in run 11, never on a real desktop); shortcuts the desktop declines, leaves unbound or binds to another key are now shown per action on the Hotkeys page (tested against the mock portal and with a fake service in `flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 16). Start with the OS now exists on all three OSes, but only the Linux XDG autostart entry is tested; the Windows `Run`-key code has only been compiled (MinGW) and the macOS `SMAppService` code has been neither built against a real SDK nor run. The tray on every OS and Windows / macOS hotkeys are build-verified only with no recorded manual verification. Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the docs/11 E56 hotkey target and the Focus, Night, ChatMix and strip Bypass actions are tested headlessly with a fake hotkey service; there is no OSD, and the Wayland portal fixes (non-blocking probe, bus loss, stale answers, GNOME and localised trigger descriptions) are tested against the mock portal only.
- **R4.5 Per-app profiles and routing (Partially implemented).** macOS is unsupported (process taps, roadmap 3.2). Windows programmatic endpoint moves exist only in opt-in `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` builds, are not validated per Windows build (07 item 2.4) and need the unbuilt virtual driver. RoutingPanel (except its red *No apps are being processed* state, wave B2) and all Windows routing / capture code are untested (`AppRouting` itself is tested with a fake router and fake captures, green on CI in run 16); Linux moves are not run in CI. Automatic profile switching by foreground application (roadmap 2.5) now exists (round 8, green on CI in run 16): the switching logic, the controller, the settings and the panel are tested with a scripted foreground source and the Linux X11 source under Xvfb; the Windows and macOS sources are only syntax-checked (MinGW) or read, Wayland reports it unsupported, and no rule has been tried with a real game. Wave B2 (docs/11 E47a, green on CI in run 17): *Automatic* no longer picks endpoint moves that a default Windows build cannot make, and process capture is gated at build 19041 instead of 20348; that gate has not run on a 19041–19045 machine.
- **R4.6 Virtual device / cable (Partially implemented).** No virtual driver exists: the Windows WaveRT driver (only its ABI header exists), the macOS Audio Server Plug-in and the native PipeWire node are designed only (roadmap 2.1–2.3, 3.1, 3.5). On Linux, the router now links the sink monitors to the device inputs with `pw-link` (docs/11 E48a, Phase 2 batch 1, local runs only), tested against recorded `pw-dump` output but not on a real PipeWire system; multi-strip mapping has no UI. The Linux sinks, PipeWire drop-ins and cable detection are untested.
- **R5.3 Neural readiness (Partially implemented).** The framework exists and is tested (`core/include/flub/neural/`: `ModelRunner`, `AsyncModelProcessor` with a fixed latency, frame-exact controls, counted deadline misses and failures with hold-then-neutral fallback, and the `isEligible()` profile rule; 13 `Neural:` cases, green on CI in run 12), but there is no ONNX Runtime, no model manifest and no model. `ProcessingChain` now has a neural slot that applies the eligibility rule at `prepare()` (8 `NeuralSlot:` cases, green on CI in run 14), and the app host can now install a model through the crossfaded engine swap (tested with a reference runner, green on CI in run 16), but nothing ships or loads a model and the slot has no parameters or UI; controls are broadband / per-channel gains only, and the neural status and counters are not in the UI.
- **R6.1 Turtle Beach headsets (Partially implemented).** Every device profile is `labVerified:false`; the device-lab plan (10 §5, 07 item 1.10) has not been run on a real headset. The Linux transport query returns Unknown; the app applying the cap on a device change is now tested headlessly (`flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11), but the banner and preferred-output behaviour are untested; the master limiter at −2 / −3 dBTP and the air-exciter cut-off are now tested directly (green on CI); Game / Chat virtual-endpoint routing needs the unbuilt drivers; the headset microphone is not processed.

Rows marked Implemented and tested can still name untested sub-parts in their Notes: notably R2.8 (the Music mode bands are not asserted), R2.9 (Gaming Boost staging and real-game efficacy), R2.10 (the ModuleSlot test does not prove a smooth crossfade between differing paths; match accuracy on hot programme, where the ceiling is involved, is now asserted within 0.5 LU by the docs/11 E37 case, green on CI in run 16), R3.1 (the true-peak bound is measured, not proven), R3.2 (AutoLevel convergence is now asserted after a large level drop, green on CI in run 16), R4.2 (painting and layout, the IEC deflection scale, peak hold and the `LoudnessPanel` momentary / integrated / LRA / true-peak readouts; its `flub_app_tests` cases are green on CI in run 11), R5.1 (progress output and command-line parsing into `batch`; the in-app Export / batch dialog's painting, and its tests are green on CI in run 16) and R5.2 (no live recording of the app's output; WAV, and FLAC in the app, always stereo; the static ceiling trim and the DAW-bounce route are untested). Closed in this pass, by tests green on CI in run 9: the LoudnessMatch unit tests (R2.1), the Width and Clarity macros (R2.8), A/B and bypass click-freedom (R2.10) and the CLI loudness-target loop (R3.2, R5.1). The documentation inconsistencies an earlier pass recorded are fixed: the Windows driver README §6 master-limiter count and its ≤ 15 ms goal (it now uses the single budget of 01 §5), the missing R4.6 row in 06 §14, the 03 §0.1 double-precision list, the stale 08 D1 note on drift tests, and the stale comment in the CD-band limiter test.
